  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Napoleon, leader of France (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: France
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Musketeer, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: SPAD S.VII, Replacing Triplane (Requires technology: Flight)
        - Unique Building: Salon, Replacing Gallery (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Improvement: Chateau (Can only be built adjacent to a Luxury resource, and cannot be adjacent to another Chateau. +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking.); Requires technology: Chivalry
        - Civilization Ability: Melee and Gunpowder Units may enlist defeated enemies, which do not consume Military Supply. +1 Culture and Tourism in every City for every 10 Military Units. +15% Military Supply from Population.
      - Leader: Napoleon
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 4
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 8
        - Boldness: 7
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 6
        - WarmongerHate: 3

        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 4
        - MinorCivWarBias: 1
        - DenounceWillingness: 7
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 8
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 4
        - DeceptiveBias: 2
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (INCREASE Shoshone urgency - Moson Kahni produced Atomic Bomb this turn. Boldness increased to 7 (from 6) - more aggressive response needed to nuclear threat. HostileBias 6 (from 5) - Shoshone escalations require stronger diplomatic signaling. DiplomaticBalance reduced to 5 (from 6) - priority shifting toward military solutions over negotiation. Shoshone now has both cultural victory threat (4/5 civs influenced) AND nuclear capability. Must accelerate conquest or face nuclear retaliation. Naval battles with Shoshone (lost 2 Battleships) show their naval strength must be countered.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Electronics
        - Rationale: Computers already researched (completed this turn), Electronics is next unlocked. Electronics allows Police Station construction, improving city security levels against enemy spies and espionage. Critical protection for high-value science cities (Paris: 346 science/turn) and production centers. Also unlocks path toward Advanced Ballistics (more nuclear weapons) and alternative energy technologies. Security essential as Shoshone (355 tourism) and rivals likely stepping up espionage. Police Stations also provide culture synergy with Iron Fist tenet.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Ottoman relationship to neutral (0/0 from -50/-50). Ottomans eliminated with Istanbul captured - no longer a relevant diplomatic entity. No hostility modifiers needed as they pose no threat with only 1 remaining city (Bursa) and their civilization effectively destroyed. Maintaining any negative modifiers is unnecessary and could potentially affect diplomatic calculations with other civs who may have relationships with the Ottomans. This signals our peaceful stance toward the remaining Ottoman territory while focusing on consolidation and cultural victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 156
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Brazil relationship to neutral (0/0 from 15/15). Brazil is capitulated Shoshone vassal and irrelevant to World Congress (only 6 votes). Resources better focused on Poland, Siam, World Congress diplomacy, and domestic production. Brazil poses no threat (1515 military vs France 7017) and won't impact primary cultural threat from Shoshone or Decolonization vote. Maintaining positive modifiers wastes diplomatic capacity on a defeated opponent.
        - UpdatedTurn: 336
        
        ### Edinburgh
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Edinburgh (city-state ally of Shoshone) declared war on France this turn due to Sphere of Influence over Palatium. Set relationship to highly hostile (-50/-50). Edinburgh only provides 1 vote to Shoshone, so diplomatic penalty is minimal. However, this sends a clear message about consequences of attacking France. Edinburgh is too distant (Shoshone's puppet city-state) to launch meaningful attacks. Hostile posture ensures AI prioritizes defensive response if Edinburgh's navy approaches (Shoshone has 2 city-states under their control: Brandenburg and Edinburgh).
        - UpdatedTurn: 336
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 15
        - Private: 15
        - Rationale: STRENGTHEN SIAM ALLIANCE to 15/15 from 10/10. Siam remains France's only reliable vassal (5 World Congress delegates, voluntary submission). Increased positive relationship ensures Siam's continued support against England shared war and Poland secondary threat. Siam provides Lampang base for operations and valuable flank position. France needs maximum diplomatic capital during England war and World Congress voting (Decolonization threat in 7 turns). Stronger Siam bond demonstrates commitment to Allies, encouraging other civ consideration while France achieves England capitulation. Resources shift from Poland to England war - Siam becomes more critical strategic partner.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ### Onondaga
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: REDUCE relationship with Onondaga (English ally) to -25/-25 from 0/0. Onondaga declared war on France this turn, alongside Sokoto. Though small military threat, they are England-aligned ally in England-France war. Hostile posture ensures AI prioritizes defensive response and signals solidarity with English opposition. With increased defenses (Antiair 90, Air 95), France can handle these declarations without disrupting primary England/Shoshone strategy.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Sokoto
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: REDUCE relationship with Sokoto (English ally) to -25/-25 from 0/0. Sokoto declared war on France this turn along with Onondaga. Both are England-aligned city-state allies joining England's side in England-France war. Moderate hostility (-25/-25) signals France's opposition to England-aligned coalition without extreme measures. Sokoto and Onondaga are secondary threats; primary focus remains England nuclear threat and Shoshone cultural victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: INCREASE hostility vs Poland to -25/-25 from -15/-15. Poland's naval aggression around Lhasa continues - Battleship/Dreadnought fleet threatening allied city-state. Poland has nuclear capability (5 Uranium, 13,891 military) and declared war despite previous alliance. Lhasa under naval bombardment with 5 Polish Battleships threatening our ally. Heightening hostility directs AI to prioritize Poland as secondary conquest target after Shoshone cultural elimination. Not full escalation yet but clear warning that Poland's aggression has consequences.
        - UpdatedTurn: 417
        
        ### England
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: INCREASE ENGLAND HOSTILITY to maximum (-50/-50) during capitulation phase. England at 52% war score with 100% war weariness; they also possess nuclear capability. Accelerating capitulation removes England's 19 UN votes (critical if Shoshone cultural victory fails), eliminates nuclear threat, and allows full focus on Shoshone. Oxford almost razed (1 population), London heavily damaged. No compromise - demand complete surrender. England's warmongering threat against France must end permanently.
        - UpdatedTurn: 419
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: INCREASE HOSTILITY vs Shoshone to -25/-25 from -20/-20 in response to Moson Kahni producing Atomic Bomb this turn. Shoshone now has nuclear weapons capability alongside their cultural victory threat (4/5 civs influenced). France lost 2 Battleships to Shoshone Fleet Destroyers this turn, showing significant naval threat. Combined cultural AND nuclear threat requires elevated diplomatic hostility to signal France will respond decisively. Shoshone's 3621 tourism and new nuclear arsenal make them an existential threat requiring maximum military pressure.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: CRITICAL ADJUSTMENT: Shoshone produced Atomic Bomb this turn - Nuke stockpiling priority. Naval increased to 95 (from 90) - France lost 2 Battleships to Shoshone Fleet Destroyers, naval superiority must be reestablished. Antiair 95 (from 90) - Shoshone has Heavy Bombers, air defense critical. UseNuke remains 95 - Shoshone nuclear escalation may require preemptive or retaliatory nuclear strikes. MilitaryTraining 95 (from 90) - continuous unit upgrades essential. Offense 95 / Mobile 100 maintained - aggressive ground campaigns against Shoshone wonder cities needed. Defense reduced to 70 (from 75) - shift resources to offense. Espionage 90 - Poland spy detected in巴黎, Brazil spy in Orleans, Shoshone nuclear production requires intelligence.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - French Sphere of Influence: Venezia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Sanction: The Shoshone:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Melbourne:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Travel Ban:
            - Description: Restricts international travel between Civilizations. Reduces the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 35%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Palatium:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Bornu:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Hanoi:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Sydney:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Brandenburg:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.

        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Decolonization:
            - Description: Enforce global decolonization. If successful, the Influence of all Players with all City-States is lowered to 50, unless they are permanent allies.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: England
          - Enact: French Sphere of Influence: Ecbatana:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: France
          - Enact: International Games:
            - Description: Begins the International Games project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : Olympic Village wonder appears in Capital. (Receive +3 Happiness and +3 Culture in the City in which it is built. 25% of the Culture from World Wonders and Tiles is added to the Tourism output of the City. All Stadiums receive +2 Gold and Tourism.)
              : Receive a free Great Person of your choice.
              : Tourism increases by 50% for 20 Turns.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: The Shoshone

  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Napoleon, leader of France (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: France
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Musketeer, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: SPAD S.VII, Replacing Triplane (Requires technology: Flight)
        - Unique Building: Salon, Replacing Gallery (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Improvement: Chateau (Can only be built adjacent to a Luxury resource, and cannot be adjacent to another Chateau. +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking.); Requires technology: Chivalry
        - Civilization Ability: Melee and Gunpowder Units may enlist defeated enemies, which do not consume Military Supply. +1 Culture and Tourism in every City for every 10 Military Units. +15% Military Supply from Population.
      - Leader: Napoleon
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):

  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 7
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: Poland is a costly secondary front with little prospect of a decisive breakthrough; by softening our diplomatic stance we improve the odds of a white peace, freeing armies and fleets for the more critical conflicts against England and the Shoshone.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Computers
        - Rationale: Researching Computers yields an additional Trade Route, which will help correct our severe -57 Gold per Turn deficit and keep the war chest full. It also unlocks the Carrier, essential for projecting air power against the Shoshone navy and English coastal cities.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: New World Order
        - Rationale: New World Order immediately reduces empire-wide Distress from war weariness and occupation, while our existing Constabularies begin generating bonus Culture and Production. This internal stability is the bedrock we need before launching the next wave of offensives.
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        - Flavors:
          - Nuke: 0
          - UseNuke: 0
          - Gold: 95
          - Happiness: 95
          - Infrastructure: 80
          - Growth: 80
          - Diplomacy: 75
          - Production: 90
          - Science: 90
      - rationale: Nuclear arsenals are ethically unacceptable and tactically unnecessary at this stage; we redirect emphasis toward revenue, public happiness, and infrastructure so the empire can sustain prolonged conventional operations. Science and Production remain high, but balanced by economic and civic priorities.

          - text:  We need to make high-level strategic decisions for France (Opponent 4) after turn 421.

They have insane tourism (3620/turn) and culture (5668/turn), and are close to cultural victory? Actually they have 4 influential civs (need 5).             - England: War score 52% positive, war weariness 50% (high). They are weaker (score 1991, military 2344). We are winning against England.
            - Poland: Score 3892, military 36788, 68 techs, 19 cities, 474 population. War score slightly negative (-2%).
            - Brazil: Very weak (score 1156, 3 cities, military 1569). They are a vassal of The Shoshone.

            - Cultural: We are influential over 4 civs (need 5: The Shoshone is the remaining one at Familiar 27%). The Shoshone is influential over 4 civs too, and dominant over Brazil and Siam. England is influential over 1. Actually The Shoshone needs to influence everyone? Wait cultural victory requires influencing all civilizations and having two tier 3 tenets + Citizen Earth Protocol wonder. The Shoshone has 4 influential, need 5 (us?). We have 4 influential, need 5 (The Shoshone). Since we are at war and they have high tourism, cultural victory is a race. However, since we have Autocracy and they have Freedom, and ideology difference causes huge negative diplo and public opinion issues, but tourism can still win. The "Travel Ban" resolution is active (-35% tourism modifier for all civs).
            - Science: Nobody has completed Apollo. Shoshone has ApolloComplete: 1 (they built the Apollo Program? But parts completed 0). Wait ApolloComplete means they have built the Apollo Program? Actually in Civ V, after Apollo Program, you build parts. So they may have started.
            - Diplomatic: We have 33 delegates (75% to win), need 44. Voting in 4 turns. We are the leader. Shoshone has 24, England 19, Poland 13. We have many spheres of influence enacted. Next WC vote in 4 turns.
            
            **Paris is building Strategic Defense System (2 turns left).**
            **Orleans built Heavy Bomber. Lyon built Heavy Bomber. Troyes building SPAD S.VII (1 turn). Edirne building Heavy Bomber (2 turns). Istanbul building Fleet Destroyer (2 turns). Sukhothai building Fleet Destroyer (7 turns). Si Satchanalai building Strategic Defense System (5 turns). Bursa building SPAD S.VII (4 turns).**
            
            **Current Flavors are heavily military/conquest oriented:** Offense 95, Defense 70, Mobilization 100, CityDefense 75, MilitaryTraining 95, Ranged 100, Mobile 100, Nuke 100, UseNuke 95, Naval 95, Air 100, Antiair 95, Production 100, Gold 85, Science 95, etc. Expansion 0, Spaceship 10.

Actually in BNW, Radar leads to Combined Arms, which leads to Computers and Stealth. So Stealth and Computers are parallel after Combined Arms. We are currently finishing Stealth. The options given for next tech are Electronics and Computers. But Electronics might be a prerequisite or parallel? Actually Electronics is a prerequisite for Telecommunications and Alternative Energy. Wait in BNW, Computers requires Electronics? No, in BNW the tree is:
            - Electronics (Modern Era) -> Computers, Telecommunications
            - Computers (Information) -> Satellites, Robotics

            Wait I need to check the exact VP tech tree, but the options say:
            - Electronics: Allows Police Station. Completing unlocks Alternative Energy, Telecommunications.
            - Computers: Grants additional Trade Route. Allows Carrier. Completing unlocks Telecommunications, Satellites.
            
            This suggests we might not have Electronics yet? But we have Strategic Defense Systems building in Paris and others. Does Strategic Defense System require Electronics? Actually maybe Strategic Defense System is from a mod and requires a different tech. In VP, Strategic Defense System might require Advanced Ballistics or something. But the game interface only offers these two as choices for our next tech. Let's trust the interface. Our next tech was previously set to Electronics. Should we keep it? Computers gives trade route and Carrier. Given we are in a major war, maybe trade route for gold is good, but we already have many trade routes. Actually we have 11 outgoing trade routes displayed, maybe more. Additional trade route is always good. Carrier allows naval air. But our naval air power is already strong. Electronics unlocks Police Station (city security). We already have some? Let's see if any city has Police Station: Looking through city lists, I don't see Police Station explicitly listed in the "Important Buildings" for most cities, but Troyes has "Strategic Defense System" and "Constabulary". Actually Police Station might not be listed as important. I think we should go for Computers to get Carrier and additional trade route, since we already have an extensive navy and air force. However, the prompt says our current strategy is Conquest with heavy military flavors. We are producing many bombers and fleet destroyers. Maybe Electronics is fine too. Wait we already have Strategic Defense System building in Paris - does that require Electronics? Actually looking at the tech description in the options: Electronics allows Police Station. The Strategic Defense System might be a building from another tech (like Atomic Theory?). Let's not overthink. Either tech is fine but we need to pick one from the options.
            
            **Policies:** Available policies include many Autocracy tenets (Futurism, New World Order, Iron Fist) and continuations of Authority, Imperialism, Rationalism, Tradition, Progress, Fealty, Industry, Statecraft. Our next policy is None. We should select a policy. We have 22 turns until next policy.
            
            Given we are in Autocracy ideology and pursuing conquest/diplomatic/cultural victory, what policy to pick?
            - We already have many Autocracy tenets: Elite Forces, Military-Industrial Complex, United Front, Lebensraum, Air Supremacy, Lightning Warfare, Mare Nostrum, Co-Prosperity Sphere, Martial Spirit. That's 9 tenets. Autocracy has 3 levels with 3 tenets each (total 9) plus opener? Actually we have 9 which might be all of them? Let's check. In BNW Autocracy has 9 tenets total (3 per level) plus the opener. We have all 9 listed, so we have completed Autocracy. So we can't pick more Autocracy tenets unless there are more in VP. Wait the options show Futurism, New World Order, Iron Fist as Level 1 Tenets of Ideology Autocracy. But we already have some Autocracy tenets. In VP, the ideology trees are expanded. So maybe we don't have all of them. The ones listed in options are: Futurism, New World Order, Iron Fist. These are Level 1 of Autocracy. Do we have them? Our current policies don't list them explicitly; they list: Elite Forces, Military-Industrial Complex, United Front, Lebensraum, Air Supremacy, Lightning Warfare, Mare Nostrum, Co-Prosperity Sphere, Martial Spirit. Some of these sound like VP Autocracy tenets but might be from different levels. Actually in Vox Populi, Autocracy has many tenets. Let's assume we don't have Futurism, New World Order, Iron Fist yet.
            
            Given our massive military and conquest, Iron Fist is very strong: vassals can't be liberated, capitulated vassals can't rebel, increased yields from vassals and city-states. We have Siam as a vassal. We might want to secure them. New World Order gives -2 unhappiness, police stations give culture and production. Futurism gives tourism from conquering cities. Since we are at war with many and have conquest flavor, Futurism or Iron Fist are good. But New World Order gives significant happiness and production boosts, which could help maintain war effort. Wait we have happiness at 55%, which is okay but some cities have negative happiness delta (Edirne -11, Sukhothai -12, Bursa -12). Actually those are occupied cities. New World Order's -2 unhappiness from Distress in all cities would help. Police Stations and Constabularies constructed faster and give yields. We have Constabularies in many cities. This seems very strong.
            
            But we also have other branches: Imperialism branch continues with Regimental System, Civilizing Mission, Colonialism. Since we are conquering and have puppets/occupied cities, Civilizing Mission is good: retain buildings, gold on conquest, +% production in puppeted/courthouse cities. We have many occupied cities (Istanbul, Edirne, Sukhothai, Si Satchanalai, Bursa). This would boost their production.
            
            Regimental System gives faster Great Generals/Admirals and promotions. Could be decent.
            
            Since we're pursuing Domination and also have a strong cultural/diplomatic position, we need to consider what helps most. New World Order offers immediate happiness relief and production. Civilizing Mission offers puppet city production and gold. Given our occupied cities are struggling with happiness and production, Civilizing Mission might be better. But New World Order gives -2 unhappiness from Distress *in all cities*, not just puppets, plus faster Courthouse/Police/Constabulary. Actually New World Order doesn't say Courthouse, it says Police Stations and Constabularies. But it gives +3 culture +5 production to those, and builds them 100% faster. Happiness relief is good. However, if we already have Constabularies in most cities, the benefit is just the yields.
            
            Wait, looking at our cities, many have Constabulary but not Police Station (requires Electronics). If we get New World Order, we might want to build Police Stations quickly. But we don't have Electronics yet (it seems). Actually if we're about to research Electronics next, then Police Station becomes available, and New World Order would synergize. But we could also pick Civilizing Mission which doesn't require a new building.
            
            Given the heavy war against England (where we're winning), Poland (stalemate), and Shoshone (struggling), we need to maximize military production and happiness. Our war weariness is 50% against England, 18% against Shoshone. Happiness percentages vary. New World Order gives -2 unhappiness from Distress. Distress is one of the needs categories in VP. This could help a lot.
            
            However, the policy choice must be from the list. Let me think about what the staff members will do with the policy. We can only pick one. I'll choose New World Order because it provides immediate happiness relief, production, and faster building of security infrastructure. But wait: the policy description says "Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy". We have adopted many Autocracy tenets. Is New World Order available to adopt next? That is 9 tenets. In standard VP, Autocracy has 3 levels with 3 each, total 9. So we may have already adopted all of them. If so, we cannot adopt more Autocracy.
